City of Chilliwack: Firearms Regulation Bylaw 2001 (Bylaw 2740)

new source bylaw open

Decision needed: Check every extracted rule against the bylaw before first use (Addendum A 5.3). Zone maps are traced by hand. Approving marks the register entry reviewed today.

  • a person checks every extracted bylaw rule before first use

Source text

Captured 2026-10-19 · source

City of Chilliwack Bylaw No. 2740 A bylaw to regulate the use of firearms WHEREAS Section 8(5) of the Community Charter empowers Council, by bylaw, to regulate or prohibit the discharging of firearms and provides that such regulations may be different for different areas within the municipality; NOW THEREFORE the Council of the City of Chilliwack in open meeting assembled enacts as follows: 1. This Bylaw may be cited as the “Firearms Regulation Bylaw 2001, No. 2740”. 2. “Firearms Regulation Bylaw 1994, No. 2105” and amendments thereto are hereby repealed. INTERPRETATION 3. In this bylaw: “City” means the City of Chilliwack; “Bow” means a longbow or crossbow as defined in the Wildlife Act; "Firearm” means a gun, shotgun, pistol, air pistol, air gun, air rifle, pellet gun, spring gun, or any other weapon from which any shot, bullet or other projectile can be discharged, but does not include a starting pistol in connection with an athletic event where blank ammunition is used.” PROHIBITION 4. No person shall discharge a firearm or Bow within any part of the City that is shown on Schedule “A” of this bylaw as "closed to the discharge of firearms". RESPONSIBILITY OF PARENT OR GUARDIAN 5. Every parent or guardian of a person who is under 16 years of age and living at the home of the parent or guardian shall ensure that the person does not discharge a firearm contrary to the provisions of this bylaw. CONSOLIDATED TO: March 11, 2025 “Firearms Regulation Bylaw 2001, No. 2740” – Page 2 EXEMPTIONS 6. Section 4 of this bylaw does not apply to: (1) a person engaged in a farm operation who discharges a firearm for the purpose of protecting crops or livestock from injury or destruction by animals or birds; or (2) a person engaged in the slaughter of livestock, where such livestock is legally kept according to the terms, conditions and provisions of all applicable City bylaws in force from time to time; or (3) a peace officer, a conservation officer, or an employee of the City, the Government of British Columbia, the Government of Canada or the Society for the Prevention of Cruelty to Animals, who discharges a firearm in the lawful performance of that person’s duties; (4) the use of a firearm or bow where the use is part of an authorized sporting event under the supervision of a local, national or international sports governing body; (5) the lawful operation of a shooting or target range; (6) the use of a Bow on private land when the person using the Bow has received the prior permission of the owner of said land and the Bow is used to shoot an arrow or other projectile at a stationary inanimate target, and the person using the Bow exercises care for the safety of other persons or property. 7. Every person listed in Section 6 above, when discharging a firearm or using a bow, shall do so only with due regard for the safety and security of other residents, visitors and occupants of the City. 8. Every person who violates or breaches, or who causes or allows to be violated or breached, any of the provisions of this bylaw shall be guilty of an offence against this bylaw. 9. Any person guilty of an infraction of this bylaw shall be liable, upon summary conviction, to a fine of not less than one hundred dollars ($100.00) and not more than the maximum allowed by the Offence Act, as amended. 10. If any portion of this bylaw is for any reason found invalid by a court of competent jurisdiction, such decision shall not affect the validity of the remaining portions of the bylaw. CONSOLIDATED TO: March 11, 2025 “Firearms Regulation Bylaw 2001, No. 2740” – Page 3 "Firearms Regulation Bylaw 2001, No. 2740" adopted on the 15th day of January, 2001. Amendment Bylaw No. 2835 adopted on the 3rd day of June, 2002. Amendment Bylaw No. 3113 adopted on the 21st day of February, 2005. Amendment Bylaw No. 3349 adopted on the 4th day of February, 2008. Amendment Bylaw No. 3793 adopted on the 16th day of August, 2011. Amendment Bylaw No. 5458 adopted on the 11th day of March, 2025. “Clint Hames” Mayor “D.W. Hampson” Clerk CONSOLIDATED TO: March 11, 2025 “Firearms Regulation Bylaw 2001, No. 2740” – Schedule “A” CONSOLIDATED TO: March 11, 2025 [Page 4: text read by OCR from the page image] “Firearms Regulation Bylaw 2001, No. 2740” — Schedule “A” ra - = ai - 1 hat > Z a at By = i i.8 Z “er a + j ay Be | 3 ss <r ' ne sg aR © 7 2% 7 *. a , \ ‘ Z aE 1 : 40s Ay, —S nie iF nd A a ea A ee —_ : mas prey - t : a : <a vie = ! - AIRFIELD = jum e be lL Ta -_ * . — - ROSEDALE * eer i t a eI the a é =| } = werows |, oy » neiwack ; 5 . City of Langley: Firearms Regulation Bylaw, 2015, No. 2961 (Bylaw 2961)

new source bylaw open

Decision needed: Check every extracted rule against the bylaw before first use (Addendum A 5.3). Zone maps are traced by hand. Approving marks the register entry reviewed today.

  • a person checks every extracted bylaw rule before first use

Source text

Captured 2026-10-19 · source

FIREARMS REGULATION BYLAW, 2015, BYLAW NO. 2961 A Bylaw to regulate the use of firearms and bows in the City of Langley. The Council of the City of Langley, in open meeting assembled, enacts as follows: 1. Title This bylaw shall be cited as the “Firearms Regulation Bylaw, 2015, No. 2961”. 2. Definitions In this bylaw: (1) Bow includes a long bow, recurve bow, composite bow, or crossbow. (2) Firearm includes any barreled weapon using a propellant, compressed air, explosives, or gas from which any shot, bullet, or other projectile can be discharged but does not include: (a) Starting pistols when used in connection with an athletic or sporting event where blank ammunition is used; (b) paintball guns when used in a licensed and insured facility designed for organized paintball games. (3) City means the City of Langley. (4) Inspector means the Bylaw Enforcement Officer, the Chief Building Inspector, a Peace Officer and any other person who is authorized by the City Council to enter and inspect property in accordance with this Bylaw. 3. Exemptions This Bylaw, does not apply to: (1) a peace officer, a conservation officer under the Wildlife Act, an enforcement officer of the Department of Fisheries and Oceans, an employee of the Canadian Wildlife service, or a person appointed to enforce the City’s Animal Control Bylaw, when engaged in the lawful performance of their duties; Firearms Regulation Bylaw Page 2 Bylaw No. 2961 (2) land located in the Agricultural Land Reserve where a firearm or bow is discharged for the purpose of: (a) the protection of agricultural crops, livestock, or domestic animals from wildlife; or (b) the humane destruction of livestock. 4. General Regulations No person shall discharge a firearm or bow within the City. 5. General Nothing contained in or permitted in this Bylaw shall abrogate or relieve any obligation of any person pursuant to any applicable provincial or federal act or regulations with regard to the discharge of firearms. 6. Enforcement (1) No person shall obstruct an Inspector in the administration or enforcement of this Bylaw. (2) Every person who violates any of the provisions of this Bylaw or who suffers or permits any act or thing to be done in contravention or in violation of any of the provisions of this Bylaw, who neglects to do or refrains from doing anything required to be done by any of the provisions of this Bylaw or who does any act which violates any of the provisions of this Bylaw, is guilty of an offence against this Bylaw and liable to the penalties hereby imposed. (3) Each day that a violation continues to exist shall constitute a separate offence. (4) Every person who commits an offence against this Bylaw is liable to a fine and penalty of no more than $2000.00 and not less than $100.00 for each offence, and in default of payment thereof, forthwith or within such time as the presiding Provincial Court Judge of the Province shall direct the fine imposed shall be recoverable under the provisions of the Offence Act R.S.B.C. 1996, Chapter 338 and all amendments. 7. Severability If a court of competent jurisdiction should declare any section of part of a section of this Bylaw to be invalid, such declaration shall not affect the validity of the remainder of this Bylaw all of which shall remain valid and in force. 8. Repeal Bylaw No. 1046 and all amendments are hereby repealed. Firearms Regulation Bylaw Page 3 Bylaw No. 2961 READ A FIRST, SECOND AND THIRD TIME this twenty ninth day of June, 2015. FINALLY ADOPTED this thirteenth day of July, 2015. _________________________ MAYOR _________________________ CORPORATE OFFICER [Page 3: text read by OCR from the page image] Firearms Regulation Bylaw Page 3 Bylaw No. 2961 READ A FIRST, SECOND AND THIRD TIME this twenty ninth day of June, 2015. FINALLY ADOPTED this thirteenth day of July, 2015. MAYOR CMhughare CORPORATE OFFICER

Captured 2026-10-19 · source

CITY OF RICHMOND REGULATING THE DISCHARGE OF FIREARMS BYLAW NO. 4183 EFFECTIVE DATE – March 29, 1983 CONSOLIDATED FOR CONVENIENCE ONLY This is a consolidation of the bylaws below. The amendment bylaws have been combined with the original bylaw for convenience only. This consolidation is not a legal document. Certified copies of the original bylaws should be consulted for all interpretations and applications of the bylaws on this subject. AMENDMENT BYLAW EFFECTIVE DATE SCHEDULE A No. 6106 June 26, 1993 SCHEDULE A No. 6491 July 24, 1995 SCHEDULE A No. 6941 August 24, 1998 Bylaw 8538 November 9, 2009 Bylaw 10278 July 26, 2021 The bylaw numbers in the margin of this consolidation refer to the bylaws that amended the principal Bylaw No. 4183. The number of any amending bylaw that has been repealed is not referred to in this consolidation. 6722859 REGULATING THE DISCHARGE OF FIREARMS BYLAW NO. 4183 A Bylaw for the Purpose of Regulating the Discharge of Firearms The Council of The Corporation of the Township of Richmond, in open meeting assembled, enacts as follows: INTERPRETATION For the purposes of this Bylaw 1. "FIREARM" means a rifle, pistol, or shotgun and includes air guns, air rifles, air pistols and spring guns but does not include firearms used for the discharge of blank ammunition in connection with an athletic or sporting event. 2. "PUBLIC HIGHWAY" means highway as defined in the Municipal Act. 3. The provisions of this Bylaw shall not apply to Peace Officers, employees of the Fisheries & Wildlife Branch, employees of the Ministries of Fisheries & Oceans, employees of the Canadian Wildlife Service, or employees of the Society for the Prevention of Cruelty to Animals required to use firearms in the line of duty. 4. No person shall discharge a firearm within or into the area designated as "closed" on Schedule "A" attached hereto and forming part of this Bylaw. 5. No person shall discharge a firearm utilizing a single projectile within the Municipality except as specifically provided under section 6 and section 7 hereof. 6. (1) A permit to allow the discharge of firearms within the Municipality shall be required: (a) for the operation of a pistol, rifle, trap and/or skeet shooting range, and (b) for an organized trap or skeet shooting event not located on a shooting range permitted to operate under this Bylaw, and (c) when the discharge of firearms is to be conducted by a person who is the holder of a valid resident trapping licence and a resident hunting or firearms licence issued by the Province of British Columbia who has produced written permission from the owner or the lessee of the lands upon which he proposes to operate his trap line. (2) A permit for the discharge of firearms may be issued providing the applicant is covered by an existing public liability and property damage insurance policy in the minimum amount of $5,000,000.00 validated for the duration of the permit. BYLAW NO. 4183 PAGE 2 (3) Any person requiring a permit under this section shall make application in writing to the Council of the Municipality setting forth complete details of the activity or event for which the permit is required. (4) The Council of the Municipality may, after considering the application referred to in section 6(3) hereof, issue a permit subject to such terms and conditions as the Council deems necessary. 7. Notwithstanding sections 4 and 5 hereof, but subject to the Migratory Birds Convention Act, the Wildlife Act and regulations thereto, a person may discharge a firearm within the Municipality without first obtaining a permit therefor when: (a) he is engaged in a farm operation and the firearm is discharged for the purpose of protecting his crops or livestock from birds or animals, (b) he is engaged in the humane destruction or slaughter of domestic livestock, or (c) he is engaged in a commercial slaughter-house operation, or (d) he is engaged in shooting on a pistol, rifle, trap or skeet shooting range duly authorized by a permit pursuant to section 6 of this Bylaw. 8. Notwithstanding the provisions of section 4 hereof a person may, in that portion of the Municipality designated as open hunting on Schedule "A" attached hereto and forming part of this Bylaw, during that part of each year when the hunting of wildlife may be carried out in accordance with the laws of the Province of British Columbia and where that person is a holder of a valid and subsisting licence issued by the Province of British Columbia and as required by the laws of the said Province and of Canada for the hunting and killing of such wildlife, discharge firearms therein without the necessity of obtaining a permit therefor pursuant to this Bylaw. 9. Notwithstanding the provisions of section 4 hereof, a person may, in the area designated as "hunting by permission only" on Schedule "A" attached hereto and forming part of this Bylaw, if he has a hunting by permission pass issued by the Richmond Rod and Gun Club and validated by the owner of the property, during that period of each year when the hunting of wildlife may be carried out in accordance with the laws of the Province of British Columbia and where that person is a holder of a valid and subsisting licence issued by the Province of British Columbia and as required by the laws of the said Province and of Canada for the hunting and killing of such wildlife, discharge firearms therein without the necessity of obtaining a permit therefor pursuant to this Bylaw. 10. Notwithstanding any provisions of this Bylaw: (a) unless authorized by a permit issued in keeping with this Bylaw, no person shall discharge, within the limits of the Municipality, any firearm within 135 metres of any school building, school yard, public park, playground, church, workshop, place of business, dwelling house, farm building, public highway, or other place where persons may be assembled or engaged in work of any kind, 6722859 BYLAW NO. 4183 PAGE 3 (b) it shall be lawful for a person to discharge a firearm within or into the Vancouver International Airport with the written permission of the Manager of Vancouver International Airport. 11. (a) No person shall discharge a firearm in the area designated as "no discharge toward shore" on Schedule "A" attached hereto and forming part of this Bylaw unless the firearm is aimed into an adjacent area designated as "open area" on Schedule "A" attached hereto and forming part of this Bylaw. (b) The area designated as "no discharge toward shore" shall be a strip l80 metres wide measured out from a line drawn parallel to and perpendicularly distant l80 metres from the shore on the outside foot of the dyke as the case may be. 12. Any person who contravenes or violates any provision of this Bylaw, or who suffers or allows any act or thing to be done in contravention or violation of this Bylaw, or who fails or neglects to do anything required to be done under this Bylaw, commits an offence and upon conviction shall be liable to a fine of not less than One Thousand Dollars ($1,000.00) and not more than Fifty Thousand Dollars ($50,000.00), in addition to the costs of the prosecution, and where the offence is a continuing one, each day that the offence is continued shall constitute a separate offence.” 13. Bylaw No. 4106 is hereby repealed. 14. This Bylaw is in effect and in force and binding on all persons as from the day following the date of its adoption. 6722859 BYLAW NO. 4183 PAGE 4 SCHEDULE A to BYLAW NO. 4183 6722859 [Page 1: text read by OCR from the page image] 6722859 CITY OF RICHMOND REGULATING THE DISCHARGE OF FIREARMS BYLAW NO. 4183 EFFECTIVE DATE — March 29, 1983 CONSOLIDATED FOR CONVENIENCE ONLY This is a consolidation of the bylaws below. The amendment bylaws have been combined with the original bylaw for convenience only. This consolidation is not a legal document. Certified copies of the original bylaws should be consulted for all interpretations and applications of the bylaws on this subject. AMENDMENT BYLAW SCHEDULE A No. 6106 SCHEDULE A No. 6491 SCHEDULE A No. 6941 Bylaw 8538 Bylaw 10278 EFFECTIVE DATE June 26, 1993 July 24, 1995 August 24, 1998 November 9, 2009 July 26, 2021 The bylaw numbers in the margin of this consolidation refer to the bylaws that amended the principal Bylaw No. 4183. The number of any amending bylaw that has been repealed is not referred to in this consolidation. [Page 5: text read by OCR from the page image] BYLAW NO. 4183 PAGE 4 SCHEDULE A to BYLAW NO. 4183 Schedule A to Bylaw No. 4183 NESS. Squamish-Lillooet Regional District: Squamish-Lillooet Regional District Electoral Area D Firearms Bylaw No. 1294-2013 (Bylaw 1294-2013)

new source bylaw open

Decision needed: Check every extracted rule against the bylaw before first use (Addendum A 5.3). Zone maps are traced by hand. Approving marks the register entry reviewed today.

  • a person checks every extracted bylaw rule before first use

Source text

Captured 2026-10-19 · source

SQUAMISH-LILLOOET REGIONAL DISTRICT BYLAW NO. 1294-2013 A bylaw of the Squamish-Lillooet Regional District to regulate and prohibit the use of firearms within Electoral Area D. WHEREAS the Regional Board of the Squamish-Lillooet Regional District deems it expedient and necessary to regulate and prohibit the use of firearms in Electoral Area D of the Squamish-Lillooet Regional District; NOW THEREFORE, the Board of the Squamish-Lillooet Regional District, in open meeting assembled, enacts as follows: SECTION 1 APPLICATION 1.1 This bylaw applies to those regions in Electoral Area D of the Squamish-Lillooet Regional District as shown on Schedules A, and B, which are attached to and form part of this bylaw. 1.2 For certainty, this bylaw applies to every person whether or not that person holds a hunting license under provincial law. SECTION 2 DEFINITIONS 2.1 For the purpose of this bylaw, the following words or expressions are defined: “Board” means the Board of the Squamish-Lillooet Regional District. “Chief Provincial Firearms Officer” shall have the same meaning as in the Firearms Act (British Columbia) Chapter 145 R.S.B.C and Amendments. “Firearm” or “Firearms” shall have the same meaning as in the Firearms Act (British Columbia) Chapter 145 R.S.B.C and Amendments. “Permit” shall have the same meaning as in the Firearms Act (British Columbia) Chapter 145 R.S.B.C and Amendments. “Regional District” means the Squamish-Lillooet Regional District. SECTION 3 PROHIBITIONS 3.1 No person may: (a) discharge a firearm within the limits of the areas defined in Schedules A, and B attached to and forming part of this bylaw (b) without limiting the foregoing, discharge a firearm on or across a highway or within 100 meters of any school building, school yard, public park, playground, church, cemetery, workshop, place of business, dwelling house, or other place where persons may be assembled or engage in work of any kind. SECTION 4 EXEMPTIONS 4.1 Section 3 of this bylaw does not apply to a person: (a) who is: (i) a peace officer, (ii) a conservation officer, as defined in section 1(1) of the Environmental Management Act (British Columbia), and appointed under the Firearm Act (British Columbia) or the Wildlife Act (British Columbia), (iii) a fisheries officer appointed or designated under the Fisheries Act (Canada), (iv) an officer of the Parks Branch of the Ministry of Environment (British Columbia), (v) in possession of a firearm for use in connection with his or her employment by an armoured car service as defined in the Private Investigators and Security Agencies Act (British Columbia), or (vi) otherwise authorized by any provincial or federal enactment to possess and to discharge a firearm in the performance of his or her duties, when engaged in his or her official duties; (b) who is authorized by a permit issued by the Chief Provincial Firearms Officer to discharge a firearm in an indoor shooting range under the Firearms Act (British Columbia); or (c) who is on a shooting range approved in writing by the Chief Provincial Firearms Officer. (d) who is a bonafide farmer or their agent acting on behalf of the farmer in rural zoned properties when engaged in a farm operation and the firearm is discharged for the purpose of protecting his or her crops or livestock from birds or animals, or for the purpose of slaughtering livestock on his or her own property, subject to the provisions of the Wildlife Act (British Columbia), Farm Protection Act (British Columbia), and the Livestock Act (British Columbia). SECTION 5 PERMISSIONS 5.1 The Regional Board may by resolution, on receipt of a written application from an approved club or organization, grant permission to the applicant for the operation of a rifle range or trap club, or both, within a closed area, provided that permission is first received from the local detachment of the Royal Canadian Mounted Police, and from the officer named in Section 4.1(c) of this bylaw, as required. SECTION 6 INSPECTIONS & ENFORCEMENT 6.1 Regional District bylaw enforcement officers and members of the Royal Canadian Mounted Police are hereby authorized to enter, at all reasonable times, on any property subject to this bylaw, to ascertain whether this bylaw is being observed. SECTION 7 NO INTERFERENCE 7.1 No person shall obstruct or interfere with a Regional District bylaw enforcement officer in the performance of his or her duties under this bylaw. SECTION 8 PENALTIES 8.1 Any person who violates any of the provisions of this bylaw or who suffers or permits any act or thing to be done in contravention of this bylaw, or who refuses, or omits or neglects to fulfill, observe, carry out or perform any duty or obligation imposed by this bylaw, is guilty of an offence and (a) pursuant to the Local Government Act or the Offence Act or both shall be liable on summary conviction to: (i) a fine not exceeding two thousand dollars ($2,000.00), imprisonment of not more than 6 months, or both, (ii) the costs of prosecution, and (iii) any other penalty or remedy imposed or permissible pursuant to an enactment; (b) the penalties and remedies imposed under subsection (a) shall be in addition to and not in substitution for any other penalty or remedy imposed by or permissible under this bylaw or any other enactment; and (c) each day that a violation is caused or allowed to continue constitutes a separate offence under this bylaw. 8.2 Every day during which there is an infraction of this bylaw shall constitute a separate offence. SECTION 9 SEVERABILITY 9.1 If any section, subsection, sentence, clause, or phrase in this bylaw is for any reason held to be invalid by a decision of any court of competent jurisdiction, the decision shall not affect the validity of the remaining portions of the bylaw. SECTION 10 REPEAL 10.1 Squamish-Lillooet Regional District Firearms Bylaw No. 90, 1976, and Squamish-Lillooet Regional District Firearms Bylaw No. 98, 1976, are hereby repealed and replaced by this bylaw. SECTION 11 CITATION 11.1 This bylaw may be cited for all purposes as the ‘Squamish-Lillooet Regional District Electoral Area D Firearms Bylaw No. 1294-2013.’ READ A FIRST TIME this 30th day of September, 2013. READ A SECOND TIME this 30th day of September, 2013. READ A THIRD TIME this 30th day of September, 2013. ADOPTED this 30th day of September, 2013. Patricia Heintzman Peter DeJong Chair Secretary BYLAW NO. Village of Belcarra: Village of Belcarra Firearms Control Bylaw No. 498, 2016 (Bylaw 498, 2016)

Source text

Captured 2026-10-19 · source

[Page 1: text read by OCR from the page image] VILLAGE OF BELCARRA Firearms Control Bylaw BELCARRA Bylaw No. 498, 2016 1979 A bylaw to regulate and prohibit the discharge of firearms WHEREAS Council may, by bylaw, regulate and prohibit the discharge of firearms; NOW THEREFORE the Council Village of Belcarra, in open meeting assembled, enacts as follows: Title 1. This Bylaw may be cited as the “Village of Belcarra Firearms Control Bylaw No. 498, 2016”. Definitions Zz. In this Bylaw: a. “enforcement officer’ means a person authorized to enforce this Bylaw. b. “firearm” means a barrelled weapon from which any shot, bullet or other projectile can be discharged and that is capable of causing serious bodily injury or death to a person, and includes any frame or receiver of such a barrelled weapon and anything that can be adapted for use as a firearm. c. “bow” means a bow as defined in the Wildlife Act. Prohibition 3. No person shall fire or discharge, or permit to be fired or discharged, any firearm within the Village of Belcarra. 4. No person shall fire or discharge, permit to be fired or discharged, a bow within the Village of Belcarra. Exemptions 5. Section 3 does not apply to a person who is: a) a peace Officer; b) an officer under the Wildlife Act; C) a fishery officer under the Fisheries Act; or, d) otherwise authorized by a provincial or federal enactment to possess and discharge firearms in the course of his or her duties, and who is engaged in, and acting in only accordance with, the lawful performance of his or her duties. J:\Files Admin\Administration\Bylaws\Bylaws 2016\Bylaw 498, 2016 Firearms Control Bylaw.doc [Page 2: text read by OCR from the page image] Village of Belcarra Firearms Control Bylaw No. 498, 2016 Enforcement e) This Bylaw may be enforced by any police officer of the Royal Canadian Mounted Police or any other Peace Officer with jurisdiction in British Columbia. f) No person shall obstruct, or attempt to obstruct, an enforcement officer in the administration or enforcement of this Bylaw. g) Every person who contravenes a provision of this Bylaw commits an offense, and each contravention constitutes a separate offence. h) Any person guilty of an offense under this Bylaw is liable on summary conviction to a fine of not less than five hundred dollars ($500.00) and not more than five thousand dollars ($5,000.00). Repeal i) Village of Belcarra Firearms Control Bylaw, 186, No. 98 is repealed. Read a FIRST TIME on April 11, 2016. Read a SECOND TIME on April 11, 2016. Read a THIRD TIME on April 11, 2016. ADOPTED by the Council on April 25, 2016. Ci Ralph E. Drew Lorna DySar Mayor Chief Adminis This is a certified a true copy of Village of Belcarra Firearms Control Bylaw No. 498, 2016 Lorna Dysart, Chief Administrative Officer J:\Files Admin\Administration\Bylaws\Bylaws 2016\Bylaw 498, 2016 Firearms Control Bylaw.doc

Bucklin Forest Service Roadclosed at km 0.8no file and section number in the PDF

Source text

2026-10-19T19:57:48.669Z · source

November 7, 2019 Notice is given under Section 22.2(3) of the Forest and Range Practices Act the Bucklin Forest Service Road is hereby closed effective immediately beyond 0.8 km in the vicinity of Second Canyon Creek until further notice. Forest and Range Practices Act Non-industrial use of a road 22.2 (1) Subject to subsections (2) to (4) and to regulations respecting roads made under the Industrial Roads Act, a road that is (a) a forest service road, or (b) constructed or maintained by the holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence may be used by any person, other than a person referred to in section 22.1, without charge. (2) The (a) district manager, for a forest service road, or (b) holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence, for a road under the permit or licence, may take action under subsection (3) if (c) use of the road under subsection (1) would likely (i) cause significant damage to the road, (ii) cause significant sediment delivery, or (iii) endanger property, public health or public safety, or (d) the presence on the road of a vehicle or animal would likely cause damage to the road or environment or endanger life or property. (3) In the circumstances described in subsection (2), (a) the district manager, for a forest service road, may (i) close the road or restrict its use, or (ii) remove a vehicle or an animal from the road at the expense of the owner of the vehicle or animal, and (b) the holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence, for a road under the permit or licence, (i) with the prior consent of the minister, may close the road or restrict its use, or (ii) may remove a vehicle or an animal from the road at the expense of the owner of the vehicle or animal. This action is in response to the current structure conditions for Structure CK-2231 identified in the last bridge inspection dated November 6, 2019 which pose a significant threat to workers safety. The current road closure may change subject to replacement of the bridge guardrail. Thank- you for your cooperation during this time. Digitally signed by Mike Peters Date: 2019.11.08 10:34:25 -08'00' Mike Peters District Manager Chilliwack Natural Resource District Ministry of Forests, Lands Chilliwack Natural Resource District Mailing Address: Tel: (604) 702-5700 Natural Resource 46360 Airport Road Fax: (604) 702-5711 Operations and Rural Development Chilliwack, BC, V2P 1A5 [Page 3: text read by OCR from the page image] Ministry of Forests, Lands — Chilliwack Natural Resource District Mailing Address: Tel: (604) 702-5700 Natural Resource 46360 Airport Road Fax: (604) 702-5711 Operations and Rural Development Chilliwack, BC, V2P 1A5

April 24, 2026 Notice is given under Section 22.2(3) of the Forest and Range Practices Act, the American FSR 7972.02 is CLOSED due to a road washout at 6.0 km. Forest and Range Practices Act Non-industrial use of a road 22.2 (1) Subject to subsections (2) to (4) and to regulations respecting roads made under the Industrial Roads Act, a road that is (a) a forest service road, or (b) authorized under a road permit or a woodlot licence may be used by any person without charge for a purpose other than a timber harvesting purpose, silviculture purpose or natural resource development purpose. (2) The (a) district manager, for a forest service road, or (b) holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence, for a road under the permit or licence, may take action under subsection (3) if (c) use of the road under subsection (1) would likely (i) cause significant damage to the road, (ii) cause significant sediment delivery, or (iii) endanger property, public health or public safety, or (d) the presence on the road of a vehicle or animal would likely cause damage to the road or environment or endanger life or property. (3) In the circumstances described in subsection (2), (a) the district manager, for a forest service road, may Ministry of Forests Chilliwack Forest District 46360 Airport Road Chilliwack, BC V2P 1A5 Phone: (604) 702-5700 Fax: (604) 702-5711 (i) close the road or restrict its use, or (ii) remove a vehicle or an animal from the road at the expense of the owner of the vehicle or animal, and (b) the holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence, for a road under the permit or licence, (i) with the prior consent of the minister, may close the road or restrict its use, or (ii) may remove a vehicle or an animal from the road at the expense of the owner of the vehicle or animal. Please check Chilliwack's Forest Service Road Safety Information web page for Forest Service Road advisories and closure updates. https://www2.gov.bc.ca/gov/content/industry/natural-resource-use/resource- roads/local-road-safety-information/chilliwack-natural-resource-district-district-road- safety-information All resource road users are advised to check local weather and safety advisories, drive to the condition of the road and watch for hazards if heading into the backcountry. Thank you for your patience and cooperation during this time. Digitally signed by Carissa Logue Date: 2026.04.24 14:47:30 -07'00' Carissa Logue District Manager

February 10, 2026 Notice is given under Section 22.2(3) of the Forest and Range Practices Act, the American FSR 8123.01 is CLOSED due to a rockfall at 3.5 km. Forest and Range Practices Act Non-industrial use of a road 22.2 (1) Subject to subsections (2) to (4) and to regulations respecting roads made under the Industrial Roads Act, a road that is (a) a forest service road, or (b) authorized under a road permit or a woodlot licence may be used by any person without charge for a purpose other than a timber harvesting purpose, silviculture purpose or natural resource development purpose. (2) The (a) district manager, for a forest service road, or (b) holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence, for a road under the permit or licence, may take action under subsection (3) if (c) use of the road under subsection (1) would likely (i) cause significant damage to the road, (ii) cause significant sediment delivery, or (iii) endanger property, public health or public safety, or (d) the presence on the road of a vehicle or animal would likely cause damage to the road or environment or endanger life or property. (3) In the circumstances described in subsection (2), (a) the district manager, for a forest service road, may Ministry of Forests Chilliwack Forest District 46360 Airport Road Chilliwack, BC V2P 1A5 Phone: (604) 702-5700 Fax: (604) 702-5711 (i) close the road or restrict its use, or (ii) remove a vehicle or an animal from the road at the expense of the owner of the vehicle or animal, and (b) the holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence, for a road under the permit or licence, (i) with the prior consent of the minister, may close the road or restrict its use, or (ii) may remove a vehicle or an animal from the road at the expense of the owner of the vehicle or animal. Please check Chilliwack's Forest Service Road Safety Information web page for Forest Service Road advisories and closure updates. https://www2.gov.bc.ca/gov/content/industry/natural-resource-use/resource- roads/local-road-safety-information/chilliwack-natural-resource-district-district-road- safety-information All resource road users are advised to check local weather and safety advisories, drive to the condition of the road and watch for hazards if heading into the backcountry. Thank you for your patience and cooperation during this time. December 16, 2025 Notice is given under Section 22.2(3) of the Forest and Range Practices Act, Effective immediately and until further notice a variance to the notice of closure of the Hunter Creek Forest Service Road FSR (6086.01) issued December 15, 2025, is given to the road users listed below for the purposes stipulated. The variance is to provide access and use of the FSR beyond structure CK-2157 and is limited to Hunter Creek Hydro Power L.P. Logging Ltd. (holder of Road Use Permit # 6086-16-02). Structure CK-2157 remains CLOSED and requires further assessment before any vehicle crossings can be authorized. Forest and Range Practices Act Non-industrial use of a road 22.2 (1)Subject to subsections (2) to (4) and to regulations respecting roads made under the Industrial Roads Act, a road that is (a)a forest service road, or (b)authorized under a road permit or a woodlot licence may be used by any person without charge for a purpose other than a timber harvesting purpose, silviculture purpose or natural resource development purpose. (2) The (a)district manager, for a forest service road, or (b)holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence, for a road under the permit or licence, may take action under subsection (3) if (c)use of the road under subsection (1) would likely (i) cause significant damage to the road, (ii) cause significant sediment delivery, or (iii) endanger property, public health or public safety, or (d)the presence on the road of a vehicle or animal would likely cause damage to the road or environment or endanger life or property. (3) In the circumstances described in subsection (2), (a)the district manager, for a forest service road, may Ministry of Forests Chilliwack Forest District 46360 Airport Road Chilliwack, BC V2P 1A5 Phone: (604) 702-5700 Fax: (604) 702-5711 (i)close the road or restrict its use, or (ii)remove a vehicle or an animal from the road at the expense of the owner of the vehicle or animal, and (b)the holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence, for a road under the permit or licence, (i)with the prior consent of the minister, may close the road or restrict its use, or (ii)may remove a vehicle or an animal from the road at the expense of the owner of the vehicle or animal. Once the District Manager is satisfied that the elements of worker safety and environmental management have been adequately addressed, they may further vary or rescind the notice of closure to allow other industrial operations to resume and allow for non-industrial use of an FSR. July 22, 2026 Notice is given under Section 22.2(3) of the Forest and Range Practices Act, the July 21st, 2026, closure has been amended, the following Forest Service Roads are CLOSED until further notice due to V11090, V10755 and V10742 wildfire activity: NAHATLATCH RIVER (6996.01) SCUZZY CREEK (8127.01) MOWHOKAM CREEK (9019.02) EAST ANDERSON (7972.02) NORTH AINSLIE CREEK (8019.01) SOUTH AINSLIE CREEK (9703.01) SOUTH AINSLIE TWO (9703.02) NORTH BEND (7549.01) UTZLIUS (7972.01) STOYOMA (7972.03) STOYOMA 3400 (7972 04) STOYOMA 6200 (7972.05) STOYOMA 6200A (7972.06) ANDERSON (8074.01) ANDERSON 13000 (8074.07) ANDERSON 9000 (8074.08) ANDERSON 10000 (8074.09) ANDERSON 13000A (8074.10) Forest and Range Practices Act Non-industrial use of a road 22.2 (1) Subject to subsections (2) to (4) and to regulations respecting roads made under the Industrial Roads Act, a road that is (a) a forest service road, or (b) authorized under a road permit or a woodlot licence may be used by any person without charge for a purpose other than a timber harvesting purpose, silviculture purpose or natural resource development purpose. (2) The (a) district manager, for a forest service road, or Page 1 of 3 Ministry of Forests Chilliwack Forest District Location: Tel: 604 702-5700 46360 Airport Road Fax: 604 702-5711 Chilliwack, British Columbia Email:Chilliwackforestdistrictoffice@gov.bc.ca V2P 1A5 Website: www.gov.bc.ca/for (b) holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence, for a road under the permit or licence, may take action under subsection (3) if (c) use of the road under subsection (1) would likely (i) cause significant damage to the road, (ii) cause significant sediment delivery, or (iii) endanger property, public health or public safety, or (d) the presence on the road of a vehicle or animal would likely cause damage to the road or environment or endanger life or property. (3) In the circumstances described in subsection (2), (a) the district manager, for a forest service road, may (i) close the road or restrict its use, or (ii) remove a vehicle or an animal from the road at the expense of the owner of the vehicle or animal, and (b) the holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence, for a road under the permit or licence, (i) with the prior consent of the minister, may close the road or restrict its use, or (ii) may remove a vehicle or an animal from the road at the expense of the owner of the vehicle or animal. Please check Chilliwack's Forest Service Road Safety Information web page for Forest Service Road advisories and closure updates. https://www2.gov.bc.ca/gov/content/industry/natural-resource-use/resource- roads/local-road-safety-information/chilliwack-natural-resource-district-district-road- safety-information All resource road users are advised to check local weather and safety advisories, drive to the condition of the road and watch for hazards if heading into the backcountry. Thank you for your patience and cooperation during this time. RoadFile and sectionPointsPlacement
Squeah Forest Service Roadclosedno file and section number in the PDF

Source text

2026-10-19T19:58:26.417Z · source

September 28, 2020 Notice is given under Section 22.2(3) of the Forest and Range Practices Act the Squeah Forest Service Road is hereby closed effective immediately until further notice. Forest and Range Practices Act Non-industrial use of a road 22.2 (1) Subject to subsections (2) to (4) and to regulations respecting roads made under the Industrial Roads Act, a road that is (a) a forest service road, or (b) constructed or maintained by the holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence may be used by any person, other than a person referred to in section 22.1, without charge. (2) The (a) district manager, for a forest service road, or (b) holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence, for a road under the permit or licence, may take action under subsection (3) if (c) use of the road under subsection (1) would likely (i) cause significant damage to the road, (ii) cause significant sediment delivery, or (iii) endanger property, public health or public safety, or (d) the presence on the road of a vehicle or animal would likely cause damage to the road or environment or endanger life or property. (3) In the circumstances described in subsection (2), (a) the district manager, for a forest service road, may (i) close the road or restrict its use, or (ii) remove a vehicle or an animal from the road at the expense of the owner of the vehicle or animal, and (b) the holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence, for a road under the permit or licence, (i) with the prior consent of the minister, may close the road or restrict its use, or (ii) may remove a vehicle or an animal from the road at the expense of the owner of the vehicle or animal. This action is in response to unstable road conditions present in the Squeah FSR. Thank- you for your cooperation during this time. Digitally signed by Mike Peters Date: 2020.09.28 13:10:52 -07'00' Mike Peters District Manager Chilliwack Natural Resource District Ministry of Forests, Lands Chilliwack Natural Resource District Mailing Address: Tel: (604) 702-5700 Natural Resource 46360 Airport Road Fax: (604) 702-5711 Operations and Rural Development Chilliwack, BC, V2P 1A5

No shape or placed point to draw.

February 25, 2025 Notice is given under Section 22.2(3) of the Forest and Range Practices Act, the Mowhokam East FSR (8019.03) is hereby closed at Bridge CK-2210 located at approximately 6.3km on the Mowhokam East FSR until further notice. This notice is effective immediately. Forest and Range Practices Act Non-industrial use of a road 22.2 (1) Subject to subsections (2) to (4) and to regulations respecting roads made under the Industrial Roads Act, a road that is (a) a forest service road, or (b) authorized under a road permit or a woodlot licence may be used by any person without charge for a purpose other than a timber harvesting purpose, silviculture purpose or natural resource development purpose. (2) The (a) district manager, for a forest service road, or (b) holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence, for a road under the permit or licence, may take action under subsection (3) if (c) use of the road under subsection (1) would likely (i) cause significant damage to the road, (ii) cause significant sediment delivery, or (iii) endanger property, public health or public safety, or (d) the presence on the road of a vehicle or animal would likely cause damage to the road or environment or endanger life or property. Ministry of Forests Chilliwack Forest District 46360 Airport Road Chilliwack, BC V2P 1A5 Phone: (604) 702-5700 Fax: (604) 702-5711 (3) In the circumstances described in subsection (2), (a) the district manager, for a forest service road, may (i) close the road or restrict its use, or (ii) remove a vehicle or an animal from the road at the expense of the owner of the vehicle or animal, and (b) the holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence, for a road under the permit or licence, (i) with the prior consent of the minister, may close the road or restrict its use, or (ii) may remove a vehicle or an animal from the road at the expense of the owner of the vehicle or animal. Please check Chilliwack's Forest Service Road Safety Information web page for Forest Service Road advisories and closure updates. https://www2.gov.bc.ca/gov/content/industry/natural-resource-use/resource-roads/local-road- safety-information/chilliwack-natural-resource-district-district-road-safety-information All resource road users are advised to check local weather and safety advisories, drive to the condition of the road and watch for hazards if heading into the backcountry. Bench FSR5127.01closed at km 16FTEN section 5127.01 not found

Source text

2026-10-19T19:57:46.657Z · source

December 10, 2025 Notice is given under Section 22.2(3) of the Forest and Range Practices Act, the Bench FSR 5127.01 is CLOSED at 16 KM due unstable conditions and debris blocking the road. Forest and Range Practices Act Non-industrial use of a road 22.2 (1) Subject to subsections (2) to (4) and to regulations respecting roads made under the Industrial Roads Act, a road that is (a) a forest service road, or (b) authorized under a road permit or a woodlot licence may be used by any person without charge for a purpose other than a timber harvesting purpose, silviculture purpose or natural resource development purpose. (2) The (a) district manager, for a forest service road, or (b) holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence, for a road under the permit or licence, may take action under subsection (3) if (c) use of the road under subsection (1) would likely (i) cause significant damage to the road, (ii) cause significant sediment delivery, or (iii) endanger property, public health or public safety, or (d) the presence on the road of a vehicle or animal would likely cause damage to the road or environment or endanger life or property. (3) In the circumstances described in subsection (2), (a) the district manager, for a forest service road, may Ministry of Forests Chilliwack Forest District 46360 Airport Road Chilliwack, BC V2P 1A5 Phone: (604) 702-5700 Fax: (604) 702-5711 (i) close the road or restrict its use, or (ii) remove a vehicle or an animal from the road at the expense of the owner of the vehicle or animal, and (b) the holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence, for a road under the permit or licence, (i) with the prior consent of the minister, may close the road or restrict its use, or (ii) may remove a vehicle or an animal from the road at the expense of the owner of the vehicle or animal. Please check Chilliwack's Forest Service Road Safety Information web page for Forest Service Road advisories and closure updates. https://www2.gov.bc.ca/gov/content/industry/natural-resource-use/resource- roads/local-road-safety-information/chilliwack-natural-resource-district-district-road- safety-information All resource road users are advised to check local weather and safety advisories, drive to the condition of the road and watch for hazards if heading into the backcountry. Thank you for your patience and cooperation during this time. Digitally signed by Carissa Logue Date: 2025.12.10 14:31:01 -08'00' Carissa Logue District Manager Chilliwack Forest District

Closure will be determined after a completed Statement of General Conformance submission has been approved by the District Manager. 1) INSTALLATION, MAINTENANCE AND OPERATION REQUIREMENTS: The holder of this gate must: a) ensure that the construction, maintenance and use (Works) of the gate is carried out to the satisfaction of the District Manager and is in accordance with the requirements, conditions and special clauses set out under this authorization, i) Placed in a conspicuous location, marked with permanent reflective material and be clearly visible to the operator of a motor vehicle from a safe stopping distance. ii) Metal chain or wire rope, regardless of any covering material utilized, are deemed to be unsafe and must not be utilized b) provide the District Manager with a notice in writing of an intention to initiate Works or otherwise interfere with the Peers Forest Service Road (FSR) at least seven days prior the commencement of Works, c) provide free unobstructed access to any person appointed by the District Manager for the purpose of inspecting Works related to this authorization, d) post and maintain signage along the FSR throughout the term of this authorization __________________________________________________________________________________ Ministry of Forests, Lands Chilliwack Natural Resource District 46360 Airport Road Natural Resource Operations and Rural Chilliwack, BC V2P 1A5 Development Phone: (604) 702-5700 Fax: (604) 702-5711 warning road users of the potential safety hazards of the Works, i) A “GATE AHEAD” sign must: (1) be located at a safe stopping distance and clearly visible to the operator of a motor vehicle on both approaches to the gate, and (2) be of an industrial standard, see Road Closure and Gate sign specifications Appendix A and B of this document, and (3) include contact information of the person responsible for the gate, (company name, contact name, phone number, email address). e) brush signage sightlines regularly to ensure clear visibility. f) take all reasonable steps to ensure that the Peers FSR remains free, clear and open to traffic during times when the FSR is open, and will forthwith remove debris, equipment or any other thing associated with the execution of Works to allow unhindered use of the FSR, g) ensure that any bridge, culvert, ditch, or other existing work affected by the Works are properly maintained throughout the term of this authorization, h) indemnify and save the Crown harmless from any and all claims, demands, suits, actions, causes of action, British Columbia and its regulations. b) The District Manager may suspend any of the Works provided for under this gate authorization at any time. c) All Works, improvements or other installations brought on to, laid or erected upon or buried in or under the Peers FSR right-of-way under this gate authorization become the property of the Crown. d) Any of the aforementioned Works, improvements or other installations may be annexed or affixed to a freehold, at any time or from time to time or may be removed in whole or in part by the Crown. e) Notwithstanding any rule of law or equity or paragraphs 2 (c) and 2 (d) any Works, improvements or other installations not removed within one (1) year after termination of this gate authorization may be removed by the Crown at the expense of the holder of this authorization at the date of issuance. f) The District Manager reserves the right to grant the use of the Peers FSR right-of- way for any purpose across the Works. g) This authorization may be cancelled by the District Manager by notice in writing to the holder of this authorization, for any breach of the requirements, conditions, or special clauses set out under this gate authorization. 3) SPECIAL CLAUSES: a) The holder of the authorization shall ensure that the gate is constructed and maintained for the safe operation of all road users. Rural Chilliwack, BC V2P 1A5 Development Phone: (604) 702-5700 Fax: (604) 702-5711 b) Access for other authorized Road Use Permit holders must be maintained, c) First Nations must be allowed access Forest and Range Practices Act Non-industrial use of a road 22.2 (1)Subject to subsections (2) to (4) and to regulations respecting roads made under the Industrial Roads Act, a road that is (a)a forest service road, or (b)authorized under a road permit or a woodlot licence may be used by any person without charge for a purpose other than a timber harvesting purpose, silviculture purpose or natural resource development purpose. (2)The (a)district manager, for a forest service road, or (b)holder of a road permit or a woodlot licence, for a road under the permit or licence, may take action under subsection (3) if (c)use of the road under subsection (1) would likely (i)cause significant damage to the road, (ii)cause significant sediment delivery, or (iii)endanger property, public health or public safety, or (d)the presence on the road of a vehicle or animal would likely cause damage to the road or environment or endanger life or property. 2026-10-19T20:00:02.133Z · source

Frequently Asked Questions – Incomappleux Forest Service Road Deactivation 1) Why is the Incomappleux Forest Service Road (FSR) being deactivated? The Incomappleux FSR has had many issues at the site of the crevice bridge. In 2006, there was a signiﬁcant rockfall event of 150m3 that damaged the structural integrity of the bridge. There are other structures on the FSR and tributary roads that are damaged and/or unsafe. The road is in disrepair and there are no funds available to do maintenance on it. Since 2005 the FSR has been closed off and on due to safety issues at the crevice bridge. It has been gated since 2017, with the road being slated for deactivation since 2021. The forest tenure holder has not been active in the valley for 20 years. In 2023, the upper part of the valley was turned into a Conservancy under the Park Act, and the lower part of the valley has been speciﬁed as a designated area under Part 13 of the Forest Act. No harvesting or road construction is allowed in this area. The FSR’s and the tributary roads were planned for deactivation regardless of these recent developments, but the creation of the conservancy and the Part 13 designation ensure that there will be no further forestry activities in the valley. Under the responsibility of a resource district, FSRs are deactivated in a planned manner that considers future access needs, road user safety, cost efficiency and values at risk. Once an FSR is no longer required for industrial use, it is deactivated. Deactivation is done to relieve the permit or tenure holder of their ongoing obligation to maintain roads and associated structures. Deactivation has beneﬁts such as minimizing risks to environmental, social and economic resources within and adjacent to roads from hazards such as landslides, soil erosion and sediment transportation. It can improve ﬁsh and wildlife habitat, and in some instances, support the recovery of endangered species. 2) What authority does the Ministry of Forests have to close and discontinue the Incomappleux Forest Service Road? Under the Forest and Range Practices Act, Section 22.2 (2) and (3), the District Manager may close an FSR if they believe that use of the road would be a risk to public safety. The Incomappleux FSR was closed under FRPA Section 22.2(3), with the intent to deactivate it as there is no funding to do repairs or maintenance, and there is no future industrial access need. The District Manager is responsible for road deactivation, under Section 82 of the Forest Planning and Practices Regulation. Date: April 2025 File: 11250-30/3397 Incomappleux 3) What is the Engineering Road Funding Policy? The Ministry of Forests has developed a road funding policy to serve as a framework to allocate funds for the maintenance, improvement and closure of FSRs maintained by the Crown. FSRs are not built or maintained to the same standards as public highways. Many resource roads are not maintained for vehicular traffic. Most resource roads have rough, loose gravel surfaces and are narrow (often one lane wide). The ministry prioritizes how funds are allocated for FSR maintenance (operating) and major road and bridge improvement (capital) projects in the following order, roads accessing: 1. communities where the road provides primary access to a larger group of year-round residents within a speciﬁc location. 2. rural residences - primary access only to a signiﬁcant number of year-round residences. 3. high value recreation sites that have been designated as such by the Ministry of Environment, Rec Sites and Trails BC representative. 4) Have BC Parks, Ministry of Water, Land Resource Stewardship, and the Ministry of Mining and Critical Minerals been consulted on the planned deactivation? There have been ongoing discussions between all Ministries related to the Incomappleux Valley. The FSR’s were excluded from the Conservancy as they fall under the Ministry of Forests jurisdiction to manage. 5) What is going to happen to the abandoned mine sites in the Valley? The Ministry of Forests has been in discussions with the Ministry of Mining and Critical Minerals and the Ministry of Environment regarding abandoned mines in the valley. Please contact the Ministry of Mining and Critical Minerals with questions related to mine remediation. 6) What is going to happen to the Road Permit roads in the Valley? In addition to the FSR’s in the valley, there are still some roads that are under road permit to a forest licencee. The Ministry of Forests is coordinating with the road permit holder to ensure that any work they need to complete on their roads will be done in coordination with the FSR deactivation work. 7) What about access for private property owners, wildﬁre response, and emergency response? The deactivation will not impact wildﬁre response or emergency response as this road has been gated since 2017 and no access has been allowed to landowners or the public. Any emergency response will need to be completed via helicopter including access for wildﬁre response. Date: April 2025 File: 11250-30/3397 Incomappleux 8) Will there be a public consultation session? How are the public, stakeholders and miners being consulted with? The Ministry of Forests understands that private landowners have an interest in keeping the FSR open for their access, however, the road does not meet the MOF Engineering Program funding policy and due to the failing infrastructure, needs to be deactivated. There are no plans to have a public meeting in relation to the planned deactivation. This closure has been communicated to landowners in the valley through posted notices on the FSR, by letter via posted mail in 2021, 2023 and 2024 and by email. There has been information posted on the Selkirk Natural Resource District road safety information website since 2020, advising that the FSR was closed and that there was no planned repair date. Additionally, there has been signs posted at the start of the FSR indicating that the road is closed due to public safety concerns. The draft deactivation plans were recently posted on the website, and advertised in the Valley Voice for public commenting and the public commenting period has recently ended. All the comments that were received will be reviewed and considered by the decision maker prior to the deactivation project moving forward. 9) If Bridge N5-125, the crevice bridge, is going to be repaired to provide access for the deactivation, can the Ministry of Forests not sign the road as not maintained and relieve their liability? The crevice bridge is not being repaired. The bridge work that is ongoing is to provide temporary access for the Ministry of Forests staff and contractors, to carry out deactivation activities. There are still major structural issues with the crevice bridge that are not being addressed. For the Ministry of Forests to limit liability, it must deactivate the road, and it must meet the requirements set out in the Forest Planning and Practices Regulation 79 and 82. Road deactivation includes removing bridges and stream culverts, stabilizing the road prism and barricading the road surface width in a manner that is clearly visible to prevent access by motor vehicles. The province cannot release liability through the use of a sign. 10) What is the timeline of the proposed work going forward? The timing of the work is subject to change depending on weather delays and contractor availability. Currently, for the 2025 ﬁeld season, the following work is planned: • Rock fall hazard and danger tree assessments including a drone ﬂight and office assessment • Rock scaling • Bridge redecking work and rock fall removal • Bridge Engineer inspection of the crevice bridge. Date: April 2025 File: 11250-30/3397 Incomappleux • Mobilize equipment and personnel for deactivation work on the west side of the Incomappleux River. This will include the installation of a tempora

APPENDIX A Incomappleux Rd Bridge Deactivation Prescription Bridge 1: N5-037 (WP1-11) Location: 11 N 455020 5630870 Description: 8 m steel structure with attached steel deck. Guard rails are steel and are bolted on. There are 14 lock blocks to be removed: 7 on each side. The bridge sits on a single creosote tie on top of the lock blocks on each end. Creosote ties will need to be removed to an approved facility for disposal. There are cut holes in the bridge deck that can be used to lift and remove. Adjacent area is very flat with lots of room for machines to work. No danger trees for removal at the time of assessment. Stream: Stream channel is 4.8m wide with low flow and low scour. Banks on the low side are sloped and a metre high. Assumed fish bearing Operating Window: Assumed fish bearing. August 20th – 31st. Introduction At the request of Michael Ferguson, RFT, of the Ministry of Forests – Selkirk Natural Resource District (MOF), SNT Geotechnical Ltd. (SNTG) has completed a permanent road deactivation prescription for the Rady FSR. Road Permit Road R07745 extends off of Highway 31 approximately 15km southeast of the community of Trout Lake, BC and Rady FSR begins at the end of R07745 approximately 5km upchain of the highway junction. The road ascends the face unit slopes of the northeast sidewall of the Trout Lake valley between Rady Creek and an unnamed creek to the north. The location of the Rady FSR is indicated on Figure 1 below. N Rady FSR slated for deactivation Figure 1 – Location key map. As per the MOF Engineering Manual, a Qualified Registered Professional (QRP – RFT, RPF, P. Eng., or P. Geo.) must prepare, sign, and seal a prescription for road deactivation works and provide the results of the assessment in a letter or report that accompanies the deactivation prescription. This report is intended to meet this requirement for the Rady FSR. 2 Rady FSR Road Deactivation September 8, 2026 2. Forest Planning and Practices Regulation – Road Deactivation The management of forest roads is regulated by the Forest Planning and Practices Regulation (FPPR) of the Forest and Range Practices Act (FRPA). The two main sections of the FPPR related to road deactivation are Sections 37 and 82 (1): Section 37 An authorized person who carries out a primary forest activity must ensure that the primary forest activity does not cause a landslide that has a material adverse effect in relation to one or more of the subjects listed in Section 149 (1) of the Forest and Range Practices Act. Section 82 (1) A person who deactivates a road must do the following: (a) barricade the road surface width in a clearly visible manner to prevent access by motor vehicles, other than all-terrain vehicles; (b) remove bridge and log culvert superstructures and stream pipe culverts; (c) remove bridge and log culvert substructures, if the failure of these substructures would have a material adverse effect on downstream property, improvements or forest resources; (d) stabilization of the road prism or clearing width of the road if the stabilization is necessary to reduce the likelihood of a material adverse effect in relation to one or more of the subjects listed in section 149 (1) of the Act. Professional Guidance and Best Management Practices The following professional practice guidelines and practice advisories are to be used for professional services related to forest roads including road deactivation prescriptions. Each of these was jointly released by Engineers and Geoscientists BC (EGBC) and Forest Professionals BC (FPBC). Guidelines: x Professional Services in the Forest Sector: Forest Roads, version 2.0 (2024). x Professional Roles and Responsibilities for the Life Cycle of Forests Roads (2022) x Professional Services in the Forest Sector - Crossings (2021). x Watershed Assessment and Management of Hydrologic and Geomorphic Risk in the Forest Sector (2020). x Professional Services in the Forest Sector – Terrain Stability Assessments (2010). x Management of Terrain Stability in the Forest Sector (2008). Other indirect EGBC professional practice guidelines that may be required include the following: 3 Rady FSR Road Deactivation September 8, 2026 x EGBC Professional Practice Guideline: Retaining Wall Design (2024). x EGBC Professional Practice Guideline: Erosion and Sediment Control (2024). x EGBC Professional Practice Guideline: Landslide Assessments in BC (2023) Practice Advisories: x Professional Roles and Responsibilities for the Life Cycle of Forest Roads (2022). x Professional Roles and Responsibilities for the Life Cycle of Forest Road Crossings (2019). Professional practice guidelines state that other related documents guide professionals in executing their work. These include: x The MOF Engineering Manual (2026), which states that “the intent of road deactivation is to place a road in a self-maintaining state that will indefinitely protect adjacent resources.” x Hillslope Restoration Guidebook Best Management Practices Handbook (2001): x MoF Kamloops Forest Region (1997) – Typical road deactivation prescription drawings and figures. The MOF has indicated that the road is intended to be deactivated to a permanent standard, and that post-deactivation vehicular access is not required. 3. Road Deactivation Plan As per the Ministry of Forests (MOF) Engineering Manual, “the intent of road deactivation is to place a road in a self-maintaining state that will indefinitely protect adjacent resources.” Where vehicle access is not required and where local slopes are unstable, road deactivation generally consists of returning the terrain and drainage patterns along the road alignment to as close to a pre-road construction state as is practical. This will typically reduce the likelihood of a landslide associated with the deactivated road alignment to Low or Very Low. EGBC-ABCFP 20241 indicates that Road Deactivation Plans must be prepared by the Road Activity Professional of Record (POR), and there must be a Coordinating Registered Professional (CRP). The Road Activity POR and CRP will retain Specialists where required for development of road de

stricter dm notice open

  • a person checks every AI reading before it counts (Addendum A 7.2: the gold set has not passed yet)

Banon Creek Forest Service Roadgatedno file and section number in the PDF

Source text

2026-10-19T20:00:46.775Z · source

FOR IMMEDIATE RELEASE Access Changes Implemented to Protect Local Watershed Ladysmith, BC — The Town of Ladysmith, the Province and Forestry Stakeholders are advising the public of new access controls on the Banon Creek Forest Service Road to help protect drinking water, forest resources, and public safety. Due to ongoing issues with unauthorized camping and illegal dumping resulting in an increased wildfire risk and possible watershed contamination, weekday access to the area will be restricted. Beginning on May 19, 2026, the access gate to the Banon Creek Forest Service Road at the end of South Watts Road will be closed Monday to Friday. It will be open on weekends and statutory holidays from 8:00 am to 4:00 pm. “We know the gate closure is disappointing for many community members who use the area for recreation, especially during the warmer months. Unfortunately, weekday closures are necessary due to the increased risk of wildfire,” says Nicholas Pescod, the Town’s Communications & Engagement Specialist. Environment and Climate Change Canada is predicting 2026 to be one of the hottest summers on record. A major wildfire within the watershed could significantly deplete the Town’s reservoir, impact water quality, and require severe water restrictions. These measures are intended to reduce risk while still allowing for responsible recreational use. The watershed is located outside Town boundaries and is managed under provincial timber licences held by private forestry companies and Indigenous forestry operators. The Town owns the Holland Lake reservoir and jointly owns the Stocking Lake reservoir and surrounding lands with the CVRD. Access and watershed projection agreements are in place with adjacent landowners. As such, the Town does not directly control access to the watershed but is working collaboratively with the Ministry of Forests and land managers to better regulate use of the area. This watershed provides drinking water for approximately 12,000 people in the areas of the Town of Ladysmith, the Stz’uminus First Nation, the Diamond and Saltair. Recreational access will continue on weekends and statutory holidays. Non-motorized users can access the watershed at any time and the Stocking Lake, Heart Lake and Banon Creek Falls trails will remain open for non-motorized use. Non-motorized users wishing to reach Banon Creek Falls from Monday to Friday can use the Stocking Lake trail to reach the Banon Creek Service Road, then head south to the spur road that provides access to the trailhead. Updated access information and maps will be shared through social media, direct notifications, and online resources. The Town and the Province will continue to monitor activity in the area and will attempt to balance recreational access with watershed protection. LINK TO MAP PDF HERE LINK TO GPS DATA HERE The Town of Ladysmith respectfully acknowledges that we gather and work on the unceded territory of the Stz’uminus First Nation, the traditional keepers of this land. -30- Media Contact Nicholas Pescod Communications & Engagement Specialist npescod@ladysmith.ca 250-268-0606

Ministry of Forests ROAD RESTRICTIONS NOTICE April 10, 2026 for the Stuart Nechako Forest District This notice becomes effective April 13, 2026, at 12:00:01 am. Bulletin #3 Be advised, pursuant to Section 6 (2) (e) of the Forest Service Road Use Regulation, road restrictions will be implemented on Forest Service Roads (FSRs) within the Stuart Nechako Forest District as follows: All Forest Service Roads within the Stuart Nechako Forest District Effective April 13, 2026, at 12:00:01am – restricted 70% Highway legal axle loading. Road users must monitor roads for damage. If any subgrade damage occurs, activity must cease immediately. Use of the FSR’s for the following are not restricted: • Road maintenance activities • Tree planting activities. • Grocery, fuel, and other essential deliveries to communities. Restrictions subject to change on short notice. The District will continue to monitor road conditions and may change restrictions. Jill Park RPF District Manager Stuart Nechako Forest District

Driftwood and Fall FSR Deactivation of bypassed road sections, between 82 and 95km Driftwood FSR OVERVIEW The Ministry of Forests (MOF) is working to deactivate a portion of the Driftwood and Fall Forest Service Roads (FSR). The deactivation will affect portions of the two FSRs that have been bypassed by the construction of two roads by Canadian Forest Products. When deactivation takes place, these new roads (road permit) will be rolled over into the appropriate FSRs. This project is intended to improve the safety along the upper Driftwood and entrance to the Fall FSR, by removing parallel road sections which are using the same radio channels. Culverts and major structures will be removed to create natural drainage patterns and prevent harm to natural resource values. Canadian Forest Products may place small sections of the planned deactivation under Road Permit to maintain access to their existing road networks. WORK DETAILS MINISTRY CONTACT MINISTRY CONTACT COORDINATING REGISTERED District Engineering Officer PROFESSIONAL Sean Baumann Sean Baumann 250-570-0672 250-570-0672 Map Not to Scale Sean.Baumann@gov.bc.ca Sean.Baumann@gov.bc.ca SCHEDULE Driftwood FSR G5-024A The project is proposed to take place during the 2026 ﬁeld season*. Funding and availability of key personnel dependent. IMPACTS ON ROAD USERS The Driftwood and Fall Forest Service Roads will remain open utilizing the new construction. Access to Lovell Cove will now be from 95km on the Driftwood FSR. DEACTIVATION WORK OVERVIEW Fall FSR G5-060 Temporary bridge structure removal 1 Major Culvert removal 2 Cross drain style excavation unknown at this time MAJOR WORK SITES Teegee Creek (G5-024A) (Temporary Bridge) The Teegee Creek crossing is a 15.2m clear-span bridge, which is located at 92.8 km on the Driftwood FSR. This bridge was placed as a temporary measure to replace a Fall FSR G5-061 culvert and as an interim measure prior to the new road construction. **No fish testing has been undertaken for this stream, but it is presently assumed to be fish bearing. Teegee Creek (G5-060) There is a 2800mm culvert on this site, which is located at 0.1 km on the Fall FSR. **No fish testing has been undertaken for this stream, but it is presently assumed to be fish bearing. **All major structures will be deactivated to all required legislation, policy Teegee Creek (G5-061) and practice to maintain fish passage. There is a 2500mm culvert on this site, which is located *The ministry plans for deactivation for the 2026 field season but will require licensees to undertake required works prior to this work being undertaken at 4.3 km on the Fall FSR. **No fish testing has been (deactivations, other). undertaken for this stream, but it is presently assumed to be fish bearing. The Ministry of Forests, Stuart Nechako District requests any feedback or concerns be provided in writing before March 17, 2026.
